The\n    #     fail-open contract is what makes these decouple: the hook\n    #     exits non-zero, the harness logs the error and continues,\n    #     and the overall session exits 0 on /quit.\n    #\n    # No capability tags; no MCP; no network — the failing fixture\n    # is a pure shell script + the harness's error-logging path is\n    # pure stdlib. The regression class motivating E14 is:\n    # shared-mutable-state at SessionStart — a hook script or harness\n    # path that writes to a fixed location (no <sid> in the path) would\n    # have N=3 sessions race on the same file, surfacing only the last\n    # writer's sticky and corrupting the per-session telemetry.\n    #\n    # E14 is structurally distinct from E3 / E4 (which pin SINGLE-session\n    # SessionStart hook fire) along one axis: N=3 multi-session\n    # orchestration. The OQ-2 contract (D-0082 §4 row E14 + D-4 decision\n    # notes) names the v1 implementation mechanism as the YAML\n    # `callback:` escape hatch — a programmatic spawn ordering that\n    # cannot be expressed in the declarative input/expects DSL. The\n    # callback (named `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:\n    # E14_concurrent_session_start` per D-0082 §4) is responsible for:\n    #   (a) spawning N=3 sessions in threads with ~200ms inter-arrival;\n    #   (b) waiting for all 3 PTY sessions to reach prompt-ready;\n    #   (c) collecting the 3 per-session HOMEs' sticky files + JSONL\n    #       ledgers;\n    #   (d) asserting no-shared-mutable-state — 3 distinct sticky\n    #       files, 3 distinct session_init JSONL rows, one per session.\n    #\n    # OQ-2-frozen body shape (D-0082 §4 row E14 / decisions.md OQ-2):\n    #   - inputs: YAML `callback:` field invokes the multi-session\n    #     spawn function (per D-4 escape hatch).\n    #   - expects: each session writes `state/auggie-first-pending/\n    #     <sid>.txt`; 3 distinct session_init events in 3 separate\n    #     JSONL files (one per session HOME); `jsonl.event_count == 1`\n    #     per session — proves no duplicate emission and no shared\n    #     mutable state across the concurrent spawns.\n    #\n    # Schema-expressibility constraint — the `callback:` field is NOT\n    # present on `evalEntry` in `suites/suite.schema.json` today\n    # (`additionalProperties: false` rejects any unrecognised field,\n    # and D-4's `callback:` escape hatch is documented in\n    # decisions.md but not yet wired into the schema). The design contract (design-spec §11 / OQ-2\n    # D-0082 §4 row E15) requires the harness to:\n    #   (a) reap the slow hook at the configured timeout boundary — the\n    #       tool call MUST complete within `hook_timeout + 2.0` seconds\n    #       (the +2.0s grace covers reap-overhead, not steady-state slop);\n    #   (b) complete the matched tool call successfully despite the\n    #       hook timeout — the tool's result reaches the agent (fail-\n    #       open disposition);\n    #   (c) emit a structured `{type:\"hook_timeout\", disposition:\n    #       \"fail_open\"}` row to `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` so the timeout\n    #       event is auditable post-run.\n    # E15 sits in `hook-lifecycle` category alongside E3-E13 — it pins\n    # the timeout-handling branch of the error-half of the lifecycle,\n    # parallel to E13's stderr-error branch. Together E13 (non-zero exit\n    # + stderr) and E15 (timeout) form the complete failure-mode\n    # coverage of the hook fail-open contract from design-spec §11.\n    #\n    # OQ-2-frozen body shape (D-0082 §4 row E15 / decisions.md OQ-2):\n    #   - inputs[0]: Use the Write tool to seed a scratch fixture\n    #     (`fixture.txt`) so the subsequent Read has a target.
